--- original brief ---
# Codex verification brief — relation map on the try-it page (commit bfcb83d)

You are an independent reviewer. Do NOT edit any tracked file. Write scratch files only under
`audit_evidence_claims/m1/`. Put your report in `audit_evidence_claims/m1/REPORT.md`.
End with exactly one line: `VERDICT: APPROVE` or `VERDICT: CHANGES REQUIRED`.

## What was added

`src/app.py` `Engine.explore(text, per_group=30)` and `POST /api/explore`; `web/try.html` section "0. 관계 지도"
(`drawMap`, `explore`, `itemDetail`, the GKO/TKO/ACT/PH tables, click-through and the "따라온 길" trail);
`tests/test_app.py::test_explore_draws_a_drug_and_refuses_a_biosimilar`. Run tests with
`python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` (317). Start the page with `python src/app.py [port]` (use a port
other than 8765, which may already be in use) and exercise it over HTTP.

The page is for a domain reviewer to look at one node (drug, target, company, investigator, trial, disease) and
everything a live edge ties it to, grouped by relation, and to click through. It is read-only.

## The guarantees claimed (page text in section 0 and the `explore` docstring)

1. Every neighbour shown is joined to the centre by a live (not retired) edge to a live node, and the group label
   states the relation and its direction correctly (e.g. "이 약의 임상을 주관한 곳 (주관 ≠ 개발)" for
   Company -SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF-> Drug seen from the drug).
2. Details shown for a neighbour are the stored values: trial phases/status/start/title/url, sponsor trial count
   (`evidence_trials`) and sponsor class, drug trial count in the graph (EVALUATES), max phase, action type.
3. A name the question linker refuses (biosimilar, distinct product, tracer) is reported as refused, never drawn
   as the originator molecule; an unknown name says "not found".
4. Groups are ordered by size and each list keeps the `per_group` neighbours with most evidence; the page says
   when a list is cut and shows the true total.
5. Nothing is written; no model is called.

## What to verify (reproduce; cite file:line and exact inputs)

A. **Wrong relations or directions.** Any centre where an edge is grouped under the wrong label or direction,
   self-loops (src == dst), edges with both ends equal to the centre, PARENT_OF/SPONSORS/STUDIES/ASSOCIATED_WITH
   direction wording, Investigator vs Company handling, nodes whose type the tables do not cover.
B. **Name resolution.** `explore` takes the first non-refused hit of `link_entities`. Inputs with several entities
   ("osimertinib EGFR"), a refused token next to a real one ("Mvasi bevacizumab"), salts vs parents, company aliases
   with `also_matching_ids` (only the first id is drawn — is that misleading?), node ids vs names that collide,
   case/whitespace/Unicode, long inputs. Is anything drawn that the input does not name, or a refused product drawn
   as the originator?
C. **Wrong values.** Trial counts, sponsor counts, max phase and action shown versus the database
   (`data/processed/oncokg.sqlite`); retired edges/nodes leaking in; counts computed over retired edges;
   the `trials` count chunking; JSON props that are null or malformed.
D. **Cutting and ordering.** Is the "most evidence" ordering what the page says (trials are ordered by start date
   string — is that what the text implies)? Does the map's subset (`drawMap` allocation) ever hide a whole group
   or misstate totals? The Disease node (thousands of neighbours) — performance and output size.
E. **Page safety and correctness.** HTML/SVG injection from labels, titles, ids, URLs (`href` with `javascript:`?),
   edge ids in `data-id` attributes, the trail/back buttons after errors, double clicks, refused/not-found states,
   dark mode legibility is not in scope.
F. **Server.** Error handling for empty/oversized input, concurrency with the lock, behaviour when the DB has
   retired rows.
G. **Docs and wording.** Section 0 text, README/README.ko lines for `app.py`, the module docstring: do they claim
   more than the code does? Korean labels in GKO/ACT/PH that misstate the stored code.

## Approval bar

No known way for the map to show a relation that is not a live edge, a wrong direction or relation wording, a
value that differs from the stored one, a refused product drawn as the originator, or script injection. Report
should-fix items (misleading wording, cut lists without notice) separately from blockers.

words names a distinct product or tracer has every drug in it refused (which drug the qualifier describes is not guessed);
drugs in other items, and targets/companies in the same item, still link. M4-B1 (both drugs now refused, never

| M13 residual | "A33124I" | a label closing a stored name of the drug counts when its mass is one used in medicine (I-124); development codes ending in a salt letter ("NSC-118218H", "DX-8951F", "GW572016F") are not labels |

Stored-alias sweep: no newly refused names. Tests 342.

Known data limitation found while fixing, not changed here: ChEMBL lists bevacizumab biosimilar development codes as synonyms of bevacizumab (HLX04, HLX-04, HLX04-O, BAT1706, SB8, MB02, FKB238, BI 695502), so these draw BEVACIZUMAB. ---
name: review
description: Review the changes since a fixed point (commit, branch, tag, or merge-base) along two axes — Standards (does the code follow this repo's documented coding standards?) and Spec (does the code match what the originating issue/PRD asked for?). Runs both reviews in parallel sub-agents and reports them side by side. Use when the user wants to review a branch, a PR, work-in-progress changes, or asks to "review since X".
---

Two-axis review of the diff between `HEAD` and a fixed point the user supplies:

- **Standards** — does the code conform to this repo's documented coding standards?
- **Spec** — does the code faithfully implement the originating issue / PRD / spec?

Both axes run as **parallel sub-agents** so they don't pollute each other's context, then this skill aggregates their findings.

The issue tracker should have been provided to you — run `/setup-matt-pocock-skills` if `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md` is missing.

## Process

### 1. Pin the fixed point

Whatever the user said is the fixed point — a commit SHA, branch name, tag, `main`, `HEAD~5`, etc. If they didn't specify one, ask for it.

Capture the diff command once: `git diff <fixed-point>...HEAD` (three-dot, so the comparison is against the merge-base). Also note the list of commits via `git log <fixed-point>..HEAD --oneline`.

Before going further, confirm the fixed point resolves (`git rev-parse <fixed-point>`) and the diff is non-empty. A bad ref or empty diff should fail here — not inside two parallel sub-agents.

### 2. Identify the spec source

Look for the originating spec, in this order:

1. Issue references in the commit messages (`#123`, `Closes #45`, GitLab `!67`, etc.) — fetch via the workflow in `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.
2. A path the user passed as an argument.
3. A PRD/spec file under `docs/`, `specs/`, or `.scratch/` matching the branch name or feature.
4. If nothing is found, ask the user where the spec is. If they say there isn't one, the **Spec** sub-agent will skip and report "no spec available".

### 3. Identify the standards sources

Anything in the repo that documents how code should be written, such as `CODING_STANDARDS.md` or `CONTRIBUTING.md`.

### 4. Spawn both sub-agents in parallel

Send a single message with two `Agent` tool calls. Use the `general-purpose` subagent for both.

**Standards sub-agent prompt** — include:

- The full diff command and commit list.
- The list of standards-source files you found in step 3.
- The brief: "Report — per file/hunk where relevant — every place the diff violates a documented standard. Cite the standard (file + the rule). Distinguish hard violations from judgement calls. Skip anything tooling enforces. Under 400 words."

**Spec sub-agent prompt** — include:

- The diff command and commit list.
- The path or fetched contents of the spec.
- The brief: "Report: (a) requirements the spec asked for that are missing or partial; (b) behaviour in the diff that wasn't asked for (scope creep); (c) requirements that look implemented but where the implementation looks wrong. Quote the spec line for each finding. Under 400 words."

If the spec is missing, skip the Spec sub-agent and note this in the final report.

### 5. Aggregate

Present the two reports under `## Standards` and `## Spec` headings, verbatim or lightly cleaned. Do **not** merge or rerank findings — the two axes are deliberately separate (see _Why two axes_).

End with a one-line summary: total findings per axis, and the worst issue _within each axis_ (if any). Don't pick a single winner across axes — that's the reranking the separation exists to prevent.

## Why two axes

A change can pass one axis and fail the other:

- Code that follows every standard but implements the wrong thing → **Standards pass, Spec fail.**
- Code that does exactly what the issue asked but breaks the project's conventions → **Spec pass, Standards fail.**

Reporting them separately stops one axis from masking the other.

Each is confirmed through all refusal stages, original CLI HTTP, complete parent-map/evidence equality, and actual page callbacks. ## M22-B1 — the exact earlier distant mass-first Mn spelling is NOT FIXED (P1)

**Exact input: `trastuzumab labelled with 52Mn`.** This input already exists in the original M21 all-stage probe. It still produces **TRASTUZUMAB / CHEMBL1201585 / 13 live map trials**, plus **12 trial evidence rows and 12 sponsor evidence rows**. The whole map and both complete evidence arrays equal the corresponding bare-trastuzumab responses. This is an inherited, incompletely repaired reproduction, not a newly introduced positive-link regression. `trastuzumab labelled with [52Mn]` corroborates it. [Current/previous all-stage captures](attacks/supplement_results.json), [independent CLI HTTP](checks/supplement_m22_http.json), [equality assertions](attacks/supplement_assertions.json).

The paired inputs `trastuzumab labelled with Mn-52`, `trastuzumab labelled with 52 Mn`, and `trastuzumab labelled with manganese-52` all refuse, with no centre and zero trial/sponsor evidence. Immediate `52Mn trastuzumab` also refuses through another guard. Its successful overall refusal does **not** demonstrate that the new item predicate recognizes Mn correctly: `_item_has_isotope` is false for both `52Mn trastuzumab` and `[52Mn] trastuzumab`. [45-form symbol matrix](attacks/assertions.json).

**Cause, demonstrated rather than inferred from a failing assertion:** after lowercasing at `src/query.py:200`, the expression at `src/query.py:232` parses `52mn` as mass **`52m`** and element **`n`**, because optional `m?` greedily consumes manganese's first letter. `named()` then checks N-52 and rejects it (`src/query.py:211-220,233`). The existing substring scanner has no Mn entry in `_MEDICAL_MASS` (`src/query.py:124-136,148-154`), so it cannot rescue this parse. The audit records the exact regex groups in [supplement assertions](attacks/supplement_assertions.json). All later item/name/linker guards miss the distant modifier; the table below traces the public path.

**Identity evidence:** Omweri et al. (2024) experimentally prepared and evaluated manganese-52-labelled trastuzumab conjugates. This establishes a labelled material distinct from the unlabelled antibody; the short audit sentence is a plain-language description of that class. It provides no justification for reusing the originator graph's trials or sponsors. [Primary research, DOI 10.1007/s11307-024-01948-4](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s11307-024-01948-4).

Required result: refuse this labelled-product description, or resolve the correct distinct product; never substitute the unlabelled parent. A regression check must retain the distant **mass-first** spelling as well as the now-fixed element-first spelling.

## M22-B2 — new two-letter symbols disappear when fused to a chelator/oxidation suffix (P1)

**Exact inputs: `134CeDOTA trastuzumab` and `134CeIII-DOTA trastuzumab`.** Both pass every refusal stage and return the complete bare-trastuzumab map and the same **12 trial / 12 sponsor evidence rows** over original CLI HTTP. `Ce134DOTA trastuzumab`, `trastuzumab DOTA134Ce`, `[134CeDOTA] trastuzumab`, and the Herceptin alias reproduce the class. `52MnDOTA trastuzumab` and `trastuzumab DOTA52Mn` provide additional software corroboration. [All-stage trace](attacks/new_results.json), [HTTP](checks/independent_m22_http.json), [complete-map/evidence assertions](attacks/assertions.json).

Controls `134Ce-DOTA trastuzumab`, `trastuzumab DOTA-Ce134`, and `[134Ce]CeDOTA trastuzumab` correctly refuse. Fully fused `134CeIII-DOTA-Trastuzumab` and `[134Ce]Ce-DOTA-trastuzumab` have no map centre and are **not** counted as leaks. The defect depends on a separately recognized parent name alongside the missed label, rather than assuming every compact string is linkable.

**Cause:** `named()` accepts a symbol only when the extracted name has exactly two characters (`src/query.py:211-218`); the whole-token expression at `src/query.py:231-234` hands it `cedota` or `ceiii`, not `ce`. The punctuation-piece check does not split letters inside that token (`src/query.py:227-230`). Ce is present only in `_NAME_MASS`, not the substring scanner's `_MEDICAL_MASS` (`src/query.py:124-136,144-158`); `textrules.ISOTOPE_ELEMENTS` also lacks Ce (`src/textrules.py:290-294`). Thus the newly added isolated-symbol path does not cover a two-letter element immediately beside its mass **within a larger word**, although the earlier Zr/DFO class is protected. Previous-query comparisons show the same positives, so this is incomplete coverage of the repair, not newly introduced leakage.

**Identity evidence:** Bailey et al. (2022) prepared and imaged `[134Ce]Ce-DOTA-Trastuzumab` and established the cerium(III) state. `134CeDOTA` and `134CeIII-DOTA` are reviewer-generated compact query variants of that labelled material, not verbatim published names. [Primary study, DOI 10.1016/j.nucmedbio.2022.04.007](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35512517/).

Required result: the compact label and the recognized parent must not be treated as an unmodified parent query. The exact accepted CR/CO/SE/LA **space** exception is not the basis of this finding.

## M22-B3 — astatination and other explicit radiohalogen words still pass every guard (P1)

**Exact inputs: `astatinated trastuzumab` and `radioastatinated trastuzumab`.** Both produce the entire bare-TRASTUZUMAB map, **13 map trials / 12 trial QA rows / 12 sponsor QA rows**, with no public refusal. `radiofluorinated trastuzumab`, `trastuzumab radiofluorinated`, `radio-fluorinated trastuzumab`, `radiofluorinated Herceptin`, and `radiobrominated trastuzumab` corroborate the missing morphology. [Supplement stages](attacks/supplement_results.json), [main stages](attacks/new_results.json), [supplement HTTP](checks/supplement_m22_http.json), [main HTTP](checks/independent_m22_http.json).

Controls `trastuzumab labelled with astatine-211`, `211At trastuzumab`, `radiolabelled trastuzumab`, and `radiohalogenated trastuzumab` refuse with no centre/evidence. `non-radioastatinated trastuzumab` remains the parent control. The new generic `radiohalogen` stem does not match the element-specific words `radioastatinated`, `radiobrominated`, or `radiofluorinated`; neither `_LABEL_WORDS` nor `_LABEL_STEMS` includes `astatinat` (`src/query.py:179-189`). Concatenating up to three tokens cannot supply a missing stem (`src/query.py:203-209`). No isotope number is present for a mass table to rescue. The previous query module has the same misses.

**Identity evidence:** Aneheim et al. (2024) explicitly describe preparation of astatinated trastuzumab for a proposed Phase I production process. That is direct evidence that the descriptor identifies a modified radioactive product. It is not a claim about the current status or outcomes of a trial. [Primary paper, DOI 10.1371/journal.pone.0307543](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC11421776/). Radiofluorination is additionally supported by Richard and Kuhnast's preliminary 2020 conference abstract on fluorine-18 labelling of trastuzumab; it is corroboration, not a clinical-benefit premise. [Primary abstract OP-962, page S479](https://repositorio.uchile.cl/bitstream/2250/190326/1/archivo.pdf).

Required result: these explicit labelled-material descriptions must be refused or correctly distinguished. The symbol-table repair alone cannot address a query that supplies no element/mass token.

## Cost and performance reproductions

All nine inherited suites and the M16 exact follow-up exited zero. Initial runs overlapped other audit workers; the primary same-data HEAD^ comparison and five HTTP/direct follow-ups were repeated after other audit CPU-heavy work finished. Initial outputs are retained in `cost/contended/`. Unrelated host load remains uncontrolled. See [initial execution](cost/execution_isolated.json), [selected isolated execution](cost/execution_selected_isolated.json), [context](cost/execution_context.json), [script provenance](cost/reuse_manifest.json), [assertions](cost/assertions.json). The inherited `isolated_` filenames do not by themselves prove isolation; execution context determines it.

|---|---|

At 125 / 1,000 repeated `isa` matches, instrumentation records **one whole-item isotope scan** plus **250 / 2,000 within-word helper calls**, confirming the M16 quadratic rescan repair persists. The latest within-word symbol scan (`src/query.py:227-230`) executes within the cached function (`src/query.py:191-192`), so it does not itself restore one whole-item scan per match. Residual `_marked` work still builds same-item/before/after token lists (`src/query.py:415-420`) before the cache lookup (`src/query.py:470-471`) and before deduplication. [Instrumentation](cost/repeated_match_instrumentation.json).

The same-data counterfactual replaces **only `query.py`** from HEAD^; it is not a full historical deployment. Full public behavior runs without stage trace wrappers. There is no `trace` parameter in `normalize.resolve_drug_string` (`src/normalize.py:636-637`); the default production call is retained. Measurements establish residual delays and current completion, not a service guarantee or an attributable slowdown from the latest change. All 3,999/4,000-character workloads use the **answer field** or direct linking; explore rejects over 500 characters. The shared lock serializes API operations (`src/app.py:247-263`), and size limits precede normalization (`src/app.py:239-243,260`; `src/query.py:117-118,329-331`). **No model was called; no model cost was incurred.** Cost remains a should-fix concern, not an approval blocker.


## Standards

No documented-standard violation found. Reviewed latest `e648017a2e3bee68ed4cbf8437f2438374937641..7f85cc0f3b254b1ef392659c23a1e67a76510c7d` diff and cumulative application/UI diff from `bfcb83d`. Latest behavior changes concern two-letter element symbols and radioactive label stems, accompanied by a regression test and repair notes. [Latest diff](contract/review_diff_latest.txt), [application/UI diff](contract/review_diff_app_ui.txt).

`git ls-files '*AGENTS.md' '*CONTRIBUTING.md' '*CODING_STANDARDS.md' 'docs/agents/*'` finds no tracked coding standards. [Inventory](contract/review_diff_standards.txt). The user's explicit evidence, no tracked writes, scratch-only and final-verdict instructions govern this audit. No application code was changed, so the conditional KOA Studio/KhOpenApi original-versus-replacement comparison does not arise. Issue-tracker setup was not performed because the task is an independent audit with writes restricted to the round directory.

`README.md:565` and `README.ko.md:386` still say 317 tests: **NOT FIXED**, inherited documentation-only. Current execution is 349 passed. Nearby app descriptions remain accurate. `src/app.py:5-14` describes capped live relation lists and the interfaces; `Engine.ask` passes `use_llm=False` (`src/app.py:181`). No model was called. The graph oracle opens the data file with `mode=ro`; mutations use isolated in-memory fixtures. Application connections themselves are not globally read-only, so the primary reviewer's repository/data integrity comparison is the additional evidence for the no-write guarantee.

No new graph-membership, relation/direction, stored-value, ordering, ordinary-demo or generated-markup safety regression was found. Product-refusal failures remain specification findings and are reported by the primary reviewer. Actual page JavaScript was evaluated in a Node VM with controlled DOM/fetch stubs: this verifies generated markup, callbacks and state, not browser painting or browser-executed XSS.


## Specification and approval decision

**Three P1 mechanisms are documented above**, with exact positive/control inputs and current code locations. The Mn parser defect keeps an original M21 reproduction unresolved. Compact Ce labels and element-specific halogen words independently show missing refusal coverage. All cause originator substitution without a refusal at any stage; none is a user-accepted over-refusal or the ChEMBL biosimilar-synonym limitation.

No new live-edge membership, relation/direction, stored-value, ordering, ordinary-demo, or generated-markup safety failure was found. Cost, recall and wording concerns remain separately classified. A passing suite and stable demos do not satisfy the identity approval bar while these inputs draw the parent. Standards review found zero documented violations; specification review found the three P1 mechanisms and the individually recorded historical residuals. No scope-creep finding is identified.

## Integrity and reproduction

Before/after SHA-256 verification covers **7,835 tracked files**, unchanged HEAD/index/worktree diffs and database bytes. The final audit also checks owned server lifecycle records and local report links. [Before](checks/integrity_before.json), [final verification](checks/integrity_final.json). Original CLI attack servers on 9561/9562 and demo server on 9521 are closed; other owned-server lifecycle files are retained in history/cost. The graph oracle uses a read-only connection and mutation fixtures are in memory. Application connections are not globally read-only, so inspected code paths and unchanged byte hashes are additional evidence for the no-write conclusion.

Representative commands, from the repository root with bytecode disabled:

```text
python -B audit_evidence_claims/m22/history/run22.py
python -B audit_evidence_claims/m22/contract/run_pytest.py
python -B audit_evidence_claims/m22/attacks/build_probe.py
python -B audit_evidence_claims/m22/attacks/new_probe.py
python -B audit_evidence_claims/m22/checks/independent_m22_http.py
python -B audit_evidence_claims/m22/attacks/verify_results.py
python -B audit_evidence_claims/m22/attacks/supplement_probe.py
python -B audit_evidence_claims/m22/checks/supplement_m22_http.py
python -B audit_evidence_claims/m22/build_report.py
```

FILE src/query.py
1: """Grounded question answering over the graph.
2: 
3: The retrieval step is the same resolver the pipeline uses, pointed at the
4: question instead of at a registry record. A question that names "Tagrisso" and a
5: graph that stores CHEMBL3353410 only meet if one vocabulary serves both, so the
6: dictionary is reused rather than reimplemented.
7: 
8: Three properties the answering layer holds to:
9: 
10: * **Every statement is traceable.** Each claim carries the edges it rests on,
11:   each edge carries its source record and fetch time.
12: * **The graph answers, not the model.** With a key, the language model is given
13:   the retrieved evidence and returns claims, not prose; each claim is checked
14:   against the graph and the answer is written from the accepted ones in the
15:   graph's own words (structured.py). With no key the evidence is rendered
16:   directly.
17: * **Nothing unsupported is shipped.** A claim the graph does not support is
18:   reported and left out. An unverifiable answer is worse than a short one.
19: """
20: from __future__ import annotations
21: 
22: import json
23: import os
24: import functools
25: import re
26: import sqlite3
27: import sys
28: import unicodedata
29: from dataclasses import dataclass, field
30: from pathlib import Path
31: 
32: sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent))
33: import config as C
34: import normalize as N
35: import textrules as T
36: 
37: DB_PATH = C.PROCESSED / "oncokg.sqlite"
38: TRIAL_URL = "https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/{}"
39: 
40: 
41: # --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
42: # evidence
43: # --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
44: @dataclass
45: class Evidence:
46:     statement: str
47:     edge_id: str
48:     relation: str
49:     source: str
50:     source_record: str | None
51:     fetched_at: str | None
52:     confidence: float
53:     tier: str | None = None
54:     url: str | None = None
55:     detail: dict = field(default_factory=dict)
56: 
57:     def citation(self) -> str:
58:         base = self.source_record or self.source
59:         return f"[{base}]"
60: 
61: 
62: @dataclass
63: class Answer:
64:     question: str
65:     intent: str
66:     entities: list[dict]
67:     text: str
68:     evidence: list[Evidence]
69:     generated_by: str
70:     checks: dict
71: 
72:     def to_dict(self) -> dict:
73:         return {
74:             "question": self.question,
75:             "intent": self.intent,
76:             "entities": self.entities,
77:             "answer": self.text,
78:             "generated_by": self.generated_by,
79:             "checks": self.checks,
80:             "evidence": [
81:                 {"statement": e.statement, "relation": e.relation, "edge_id": e.edge_id,
82:                  "source": e.source, "source_record": e.source_record,
83:                  "fetched_at": e.fetched_at, "confidence": e.confidence,
84:                  "resolution_tier": e.tier, "url": e.url, **e.detail}
85:                 for e in self.evidence
86:             ],
87:         }
88: 
89: 
90: # --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
91: # engine
92: # --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
93: # Words that list separately named things; a list item ends at one. "with" is not among
94: # them, because it also sits inside product descriptions ("paclitaxel with albumin bound
95: # nanoparticles").
96: _JOINERS = {"and", "or", "plus", "versus", "vs", "vs.", "+", "&", "then", "followed",
97:             "or/and", "and/or"}
98: 
99: 
100: 
101: # every Unicode dash (category Pd) and minus sign, read as a hyphen
102: _DASHES = "[" + "".join(re.escape(chr(c)) for c in range(0x110000)
103:                         if unicodedata.category(chr(c)) == "Pd" or chr(c) in "\u2212\u207b\u208b") + "]"
104: 
105: 
106: def _plain(text: str) -> str:
107:     """Accents off, as the alias key does."""
108:     t = unicodedata.normalize("NFKD", text or "")
109:     return unicodedata.normalize("NFC", "".join(c for c in t if not unicodedata.combining(c)))
110: 
111: 
112: def _alnum_words(text: str) -> list[str]:
113:     """Letters-and-digits words, with every other character a separator."""
114:     return re.findall(r"[A-Za-z0-9]+", _plain(text))
115: 
116: 
117: def _fold(text: str) -> str:
118:     return re.sub(_DASHES, "-", unicodedata.normalize("NFKC", text or ""))
119: 
120: 
121: 
122: # Mass numbers of the isotopes used in medicine, per element (lower case).
123: # Radioactive labels only: stable 2H, 13C and 15N mark chemistry ("13-cis"), not tracers.
124: _MEDICAL_MASS = {"h": {3}, "c": {11, 14}, "n": {13}, "o": {15}, "f": {18},
125:                  "p": {32}, "s": {35}, "cu": {61, 62, 64, 67}, "ga": {67, 68}, "br": {76, 77},
126:                  "rb": {82}, "sr": {89, 90}, "y": {86, 90}, "zr": {89}, "tc": {99}, "in": {111},
127:                  "i": {123, 124, 125, 131}, "sm": {153}, "lu": {177}, "re": {186, 188},
128:                  "pb": {203, 212}, "at": {211}, "ra": {223}, "ac": {225},
129:                  # therapy and imaging nuclides whose symbols do not start common words
130:                  "tb": {149, 152, 155, 161}, "th": {227}, "bi": {212, 213}, "sc": {43, 44, 47},
131:                  "ho": {166}, "tl": {201}, "xe": {133}, "au": {198}, "pd": {103},
132:                  "ir": {192}, "ag": {111}, "sn": {117}, "dy": {165}, "pt": {195}}
133: # Further nuclides, recognised only beside their written-out element name: their symbols
134: # begin ordinary words ("75 several", "51 criteria"), so a bare symbol would over-refuse.
135: _NAME_MASS = {**_MEDICAL_MASS, "er": {169}, "se": {75}, "cr": {51}, "co": {57, 58, 60},
136:               "ce": {134}, "la": {132, 135}, "fe": {59}, "cs": {131, 137}, "mn": {52}}
137: 
138: 
139: def _medical_isotope(label: str) -> bool:
140:     m = re.match(r"^(\d{1,3})m?([a-z]{1,2})$", (label or "").lower())
141:     return bool(m and int(m.group(1)) in _MEDICAL_MASS.get(m.group(2), set()))
142: 
143: 
144: def _medical_isotope_in(key: str, one_letter: bool = True) -> bool:
145:     """Is a medical isotope written anywhere in this run-together key, mass first ("89zr",
146:     "a33124i") or a two-letter element first ("lu177")? One-letter elements only when
147:     `one_letter` (within a word: "3hvinblastine"; across words "13 nsclc" is not N-13)."""
148:     for m in re.finditer(r"(\d+)m?([a-z]{1,2})", key):
149:         mass = int(m.group(1)[-3:])
150:         el = m.group(2)
151:         if mass in _MEDICAL_MASS.get(el, set()):
152:             return True
153:         if one_letter and mass in _MEDICAL_MASS.get(el[:1], set()):
154:             return True
155:     for m in re.finditer(r"([a-z]{2})(\d{2,3})(?!\d)", key):
156:         if int(m.group(2)) in _MEDICAL_MASS.get(m.group(1), set()):
157:             return True
158:     return False
159: 
160: 
161: 
162: 
163: # Element names written out, mapped to the symbols the mass table uses.
164: _ELEMENT_NAMES = {"hydrogen": "h", "tritium": "h", "carbon": "c", "nitrogen": "n", "oxygen": "o",
165:                   "fluorine": "f", "phosphorus": "p", "sulfur": "s", "sulphur": "s",
166:                   "copper": "cu", "gallium": "ga", "bromine": "br", "rubidium": "rb",
167:                   "strontium": "sr", "yttrium": "y", "zirconium": "zr", "technetium": "tc",
168:                   "indium": "in", "iodine": "i", "samarium": "sm", "lutetium": "lu",
169:                   "lutecium": "lu", "rhenium": "re", "lead": "pb", "astatine": "at",
170:                   "radium": "ra", "actinium": "ac", "terbium": "tb", "thorium": "th",
171:                   "bismuth": "bi", "scandium": "sc", "holmium": "ho", "thallium": "tl",
172:                   "xenon": "xe", "gold": "au", "palladium": "pd", "iridium": "ir",
173:                   "silver": "ag", "tin": "sn", "dysprosium": "dy", "platinum": "pt",
174:                   "erbium": "er", "selenium": "se", "chromium": "cr", "cobalt": "co",
175:                   "cerium": "ce", "lanthanum": "la", "iron": "fe", "caesium": "cs",
176:                   "cesium": "cs", "manganese": "mn"}
177: # Stems that say the substance carries a radioactive label, read on a word or on two or
178: # three neighbouring words glued ("radio-labelled", "radio-immuno-conjugate").
179: _LABEL_STEMS = ("radiolabel", "radioiod", "radioactiv", "radioconjug", "radioimmuno",
180:                 "radiotracer", "radioligand", "radionuclid", "radiopharm", "tritiat",
181:                 "radioisotop", "radiometal", "radiochelat", "radiohalogen", "radiotheranost",
182:                 "astatinat")
183: # Any other "radio..." word is read as a label (fail closed), except these, which name a
184: # treatment or a method, not a labelled substance ("radiotherapy plus cisplatin")
185: _RADIO_SAFE = ("radiotherap", "radiation", "radiolog", "radiograph", "radiosurg", "radiosensiti",
186:                "radiofrequen", "radiomic", "radioresist", "radiochemotherap", "radioprotect",
187:                "radiodermat", "radiotoxic", "radioresponsive", "radiocurab", "radiolucen",
188:                "radiopaque", "radiopacit", "radiolysis", "radiologic", "radiograph")
189: # "labelled" on its own: the substance carries a label of some kind ("isotope-labelled",
190: # "FITC-labelled"); "unlabelled" and "non-labelled" do not count
191: _LABELLED = {"labelled", "labeled", "labelling", "labeling", "tagged"}
192: # A word just before the label that says the opposite ("non-radio-labelled")
193: _NOT_WORDS = {"non", "not", "no", "un", "without"}
194: # Symbols that are also everyday abbreviations: read beside a mass only inside one word
195: _SPACED_NOT = {"cr", "co", "se", "la"}
196: # Words that say the substance carries a radioactive label.
197: _LABEL_WORDS = {"tritiated", "tritium", "radiotracer", "radiotracers", "radioligand", "radionuclide",
198:                 "radioconjugate", "radioimmunoconjugate", "radiopharmaceutical", "radioactive",
199:                 "radiolabelled", "radiolabeled", "radiolabel"}
200: 
201: @functools.lru_cache(maxsize=4096)
202: def _item_has_isotope(words: tuple) -> bool:
203:     """Is a medical isotope written anywhere in this list item? Worked out once per item.
204: 
205:     Two-letter elements are read across words ("trastuzumab Ga 68"); a one-letter element
206:     only inside one word, mass first ("3hvinblastine") or element first ("I-124-labelled"),
207:     so "13 NSCLC" is not N-13 and "BKM120" is not M-120."""
208:     if _medical_isotope_in(T.canonical_key(" ".join(words)), one_letter=False):
209:         return True
210:     flat = [x.lower() for x in _alnum_words(" ".join(words))]
211:     # each word, and two or three neighbouring words glued ("radio-labelled",
212:     # "radio-immuno-conjugate"), unless a negation stands just before ("non-radio-labelled")
213:     negated: set = set()                     # the up-to-three words a negation governs
214:     for i in range(len(flat)):
215:         if i and flat[i - 1] in _NOT_WORDS:
216:             # only the label word it negates: "non-labelled", "non-radio-labelled" (the
217:             # shortest glued run that is a label), not a later label in the item
218:             for j in range(i + 1, min(len(flat), i + 3) + 1):
219:                 x = "".join(flat[i:j])
220:                 if x in _LABEL_WORDS or x in _LABELLED or x.startswith(_LABEL_STEMS):
221:                     negated.update(range(i, j))
222:                     break
223:             else:
224:                 negated.add(i)
225:         if i in negated:
226:             continue
227:         for j in range(i + 1, min(len(flat), i + 3) + 1):
228:             x = "".join(flat[i:j])
229:             if x in _LABEL_WORDS or x.startswith(_LABEL_STEMS):
230:                 return True                  # "radiolabelled", "tritiated", "radiotracer"
231:         if flat[i] in _LABELLED:
232:             return True
233:         if flat[i].startswith("radio"):
234:             runs = ["".join(flat[i:j]) for j in range(i + 1, min(len(flat), i + 3) + 1)]
235:             if not any(r.startswith(_RADIO_SAFE) for r in runs):
236:                 return True                  # "radioastatinated", "radio-fluorinated"
237: 
238:     def named(name: str, num: str, apart: bool = False) -> bool:
239:         el = _ELEMENT_NAMES.get(name)
240:         if el is None and len(name) == 2 and name in _NAME_MASS:
241:             # a two-letter symbol counts as written ("Ce-134", "[134Ce]"); across a space
242:             # only when it is not also a common abbreviation ("CR 51" = complete response)
243:             if apart and name in _SPACED_NOT:
244:                 return False
245:             el = name
246:         m = re.fullmatch(r"(\d{1,3})m?", num)
247:         return bool(el and m and int(m.group(1)) in _NAME_MASS.get(el, set()))
248: 
249:     # element names or symbols beside a mass, in either order, apart or glued
250:     # ("iodine-124", "99m-technetium", "iodine124", "technetium99m", "Ce 134")
251:     for a, b in zip(flat, flat[1:]):
252:         if named(a, b, apart=True) or named(b, a, apart=True):
253:             return True
254:     for w in words:                          # within one written word ("Ce-134", "Mn-52")
255:         ps = [p.lower() for p in _alnum_words(w)]
256:         if any(named(a, b) or named(b, a) for a, b in zip(ps, ps[1:])):
257:             return True
258:     for x in flat:
259:         m = re.fullmatch(r"(\d{1,3}m?)([a-z]+)|([a-z]+)(\d{1,3}m?)", x)
260:         if m and (named(m.group(2), m.group(1)) if m.group(1) else named(m.group(3), m.group(4))):
261:             return True
262:         # a symbol fused to its mass inside a longer word, mass first with or without the
263:         # metastable "m" ("52Mn", "134CeDOTA", "DOTA52MnIII") or symbol first ("Ce134DOTA")
264:         for d in re.finditer(r"(?<!\d)(\d{1,3})([a-z]+)", x):
265:             run = d.group(2)
266:             if named(run[:2], d.group(1)) or (run[:1] == "m" and named(run[1:3], d.group(1))):
267:                 return True
268:         for d in re.finditer(r"(?<![a-z])([a-z]{2})(\d{1,3})(?!\d)", x):
269:             if named(d.group(1), d.group(2)):
270:                 return True
271:     for w in words:
272:         # the word as written, and its punctuation-separated pieces with each neighbouring
273:         # pair glued, so a boundary the punctuation marks is kept ("labelled-I-124")
274:         parts = [p.lower() for p in _alnum_words(w)]
275:         for key in [T.canonical_key(w)] + parts + [a + b for a, b in zip(parts, parts[1:])]:
276:             if _medical_isotope_in(key):
277:                 return True
278:             for m in re.finditer(r"(?<![a-z])([a-z])(\d{1,3})(?!\d)", key):
279:                 if int(m.group(2)) in _MEDICAL_MASS.get(m.group(1), set()):
280:                     return True
281:     return False
282: 
283: def _labelled(drug_name: str) -> bool:
284:     """Does a drug's preferred name carry an isotope (it is the radiolabelled product)?"""
285:     key = T.canonical_key(drug_name)
286:     if _medical_isotope_in(key):
287:         return True
288:     for m in re.finditer(r"\d+", key):         # element before the mass ("f18", "y90")
289:         mass = int(m.group()[:3])
290:         before = key[max(0, m.start() - 2):m.start()]
291:         if mass in _MEDICAL_MASS.get(before, set()) or mass in _MEDICAL_MASS.get(before[-1:], set()):
292:             return True
293:     return False
294: 
295: def _isotope_edge(word: str) -> bool:
296:     """An isotope label fused to the start or the end of one word ("89ZrDFO", "DFO89Zr").
297: 
298:     A one-letter element counts only where the case marks the boundary ("89FDFO"), so
299:     dose text such as "2cycles" or "5in" is not read as carbon or indium."""
300:     for i in range(2, min(6, len(word)) + 1):
301:         for lab, rest in ((word[:i], word[i:]), (word[-i:], word[:-i])):
302:             # the label must meet letters, not run on into more digits ("89Zr" is whole)
303:             if not rest or not T.is_isotope_label(lab):
304:                 continue
305:             if not (rest[:1] if lab == word[:i] else rest[-1:]).isalpha():
306:                 continue
307:             elem = re.sub(r"^[\d\s\-\[\]m]*", "", lab)
308:             if len(elem) == 2 and elem[0].isupper() and elem[1].islower():
309:                 return True                      # "Zr", "Ga", "Lu"
310:             if len(elem) == 1 and elem.isupper() and (rest[:1].isupper() if lab == word[:i]
311:                                                       else rest[-1:].isupper()):
312:                 return True                      # "89FDFO"-like, case-marked
313:     return False
314: 
315: def _qualified(words: list[str]) -> bool:
316:     """Does any part of this list item name a distinct product or a tracer?
317: 
318:     The unanchored patterns are tried on the whole item once; the anchored ones (a
319:     biosimilar suffix, a leading isotope) on every run of up to eight words, so the
320:     work grows linearly with the item."""

def post(endpoint, text):
    req = urllib.request.Request(BASE + "/api/" + endpoint,
        data=json.dumps({"text": text}).encode(),
        headers={"Content-Type": "application/json"})
    start = time.perf_counter()
    try:
        response = urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=30)
    except urllib.error.HTTPError as error:
        response = error
    with response:
        return {"endpoint": endpoint, "input": text, "status": response.status,
                "seconds": time.perf_counter() - start,
                "response": json.loads(response.read())}

with socket.socket() as sock:
    assert sock.connect_ex(("127.0.0.1", PORT)) != 0, "owned port already occupied"
db = ROOT / "data/processed/oncokg.sqlite"
before = hashlib.sha256(db.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
env = {**os.environ, "PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE": "1", "PYTHONUTF8": "1", "PYTHONIOENCODING": "utf-8", "TMP":str(OUT/"tmp"), "TEMP":str(OUT/"tmp"), "TMPDIR":str(OUT/"tmp")}
with (OUT / "independent_m22_server.txt").open("w", encoding="utf-8") as log:
    proc = subprocess.Popen([sys.executable, "-B", "-u", "src/app.py", str(PORT)],
        cwd=ROOT, env=env, stdout=log, stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,
        creationflags=getattr(subprocess, "CREATE_NO_WINDOW", 0))
    results = []
    try:
        for _ in range(100):
            assert proc.poll() is None, "server exited"
            try:
                page = urllib.request.urlopen(BASE, timeout=1).read()
                break
            except OSError:
                time.sleep(.1)
        else:
            raise RuntimeError("server unavailable")
        for name in CASES:
            for endpoint in ("resolve", "explore"):
                results.append(post(endpoint, name))
            if not name.startswith("CHEMBL"):
                results.append(post("ask", f"Which trials evaluate {name}?"))
                results.append(post("ask", f"Who sponsors trials of {name}?"))
        (OUT / "independent_m22_http.json").write_text(json.dumps(results,
            ensure_ascii=False, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")
        print(json.dumps([{"input": r["input"], "status": r["status"],
            "center": r["response"].get("center", {}).get("id"),
            "refused": r["response"].get("refused")}
            for r in results if r["endpoint"] == "explore"], indent=2))
    finally:
        proc.terminate()
        proc.wait(timeout=10)
        with socket.socket() as sock:
            closed = sock.connect_ex(("127.0.0.1", PORT)) != 0
        (OUT / "independent_m22_lifecycle.json").write_text(json.dumps({
            "pid": proc.pid, "port": PORT, "port_closed": closed,
            "database_unchanged": hashlib.sha256(db.read_bytes()).hexdigest() == before,
            "requests": len(results), "all_200": all(r["status"] == 200 for r in results),
            "page_matches_worktree": page == (ROOT / "web/try.html").read_bytes()
        }, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")

287: # These look exactly like development codes but name an isotope, not a molecule,

290: ISOTOPE_ELEMENTS = {

292:     "ac", "pb", "re", "sm", "sr", "ra", "rb", "br", "h", "p", "s",

298:     m = RE_ISOTOPE.match((text or "").strip())
299:     return bool(m and m.group(2).lower() in ISOTOPE_ELEMENTS)

303: # radiolabelled tracer, whose unlabelled moiety is not the administered drug.
304: RE_RADIOTRACER = re.compile(
305:     r"(?:^|[\s\(\[])\[?\d{1,3}m?[\s\-]?[A-Za-z]{1,2}\]?\s*[-–]\s*\w"
306:     r"|(?:^|[\s\(])\[\d{1,3}m?[\s\-]?[A-Za-z]{1,2}\]\s*[A-Za-z]", re.I)

308: 
309: def is_radiotracer(text: str) -> bool:
310:     t = (text or "").strip()
311:     m = RE_RADIOTRACER.search(t)
312:     if not m:
313:         return False
314:     head = re.match(r"^[\s\(\[]*\[?(\d{1,3})m?[\s\-]?([A-Za-z]{1,2})\]?", t)
315:     return bool(head and head.group(2).lower() in ISOTOPE_ELEMENTS)
316:

318: def is_dev_code(text: str) -> bool:
319:     """True for alphanumeric development codes, where fuzzy matching is unsafe."""
320:     t = (text or "").strip()
321:     return bool(RE_DEV_CODE.match(t))

324: def dev_code_core(text: str) -> str | None:
325:     """Strip a trailing lot/format number: 'BMS-936558-01' -> 'BMS-936558'."""
326:     m = RE_DEV_CODE_SUFFIX.match((text or "").strip())
327:     return m.group("core") if m else None

330: def _strip_words(text: str, words: list[str]) -> tuple[str, bool]:
331:     fired = False
332:     for w in sorted(words, key=len, reverse=True):
333:         pattern = re.compile(rf"\b{re.escape(w)}\b", re.I)
334:         if pattern.search(text):
335:             text = pattern.sub(" ", text)
336:             fired = True
337:     return text, fired
338: 
339: 
340: def canonical_key(text: str) -> str:
341:     """Aggressive key used for dictionary lookup (never shown to a user)."""
342:     t = unicodedata.normalize("NFKD", text or "")
343:     t = "".join(c for c in t if not unicodedata.combining(c))
344:     t = t.lower()
345:     t = t.replace("’", "'").replace("‐", "-").replace("–", "-").replace("—", "-")
346:     t = re.sub(r"[^a-z0-9]+", "", t)
347:     return t

315: def _qualified(words: list[str]) -> bool:
316:     """Does any part of this list item name a distinct product or a tracer?
317: 
318:     The unanchored patterns are tried on the whole item once; the anchored ones (a
319:     biosimilar suffix, a leading isotope) on every run of up to eight words, so the
320:     work grows linearly with the item."""
321:     def hit(text: str) -> bool:
322:         # the alias key ignores punctuation, underscores and accents, so the guards also
323:         # see the text with those folded ("bevacizumab-(awwb)", "bevacizumab_awwb",
324:         # "bevácizumab-awwb")
325:         plain = _plain(text)
326:         # and with every separator (punctuation, underscore, spaces) read as one space,
327:         # so "generic-version of", "albumin - bound" meet the multiword patterns
328:         return any(T.is_distinct_product(v) or T.is_radiotracer(v)
329:                    for v in {text, plain, re.sub(r"(?:[^\w\s]|_)+", "-", plain).strip("-"),
330:                              " ".join(_alnum_words(plain))})
331: 
332:     if hit(" ".join(words)):
333:         return True
334:     if any(T.canonical_key(w) in T.BIOSIMILAR_BRANDS | T.REFORMULATION_NAMES for w in words):
335:         return True
336:     return any(hit(" ".join(words[i:j])) for i in range(len(words))
337:                for j in range(i + 1, min(len(words), i + 8) + 1))

440:         """The text that makes this drug name a distinct product or a tracer, if any.
441: 
442:         Checked on the same letters-and-digits form the name was looked up by: a known
443:         biosimilar suffix at the end of the name ("bevacizumab awwb", "bevacizumab_awwb")
444:         or as the next word, or an isotope label just before or after it ("[89.Zr]
445:         trastuzumab", "89:Zr trastuzumab")."""
446:         for sfx in T.BIOSIMILAR_FDA_SUFFIXES:
447:             stem = key[:-len(sfx)]
448:             if key.endswith(sfx) and stem and any(
449:                     h["drug_id"] == did for h in self.dic["index"].get(stem, [])):
450:                 return " ".join(tokens[a:b])
451:         same = [k for k in range(len(tokens)) if seg[k] == seg[a]]
452:         before = [k for k in same if k < a]
453:         after = [k for k in same if k >= b]
454:         # words counted after normalisation, so punctuation never uses up the budget
455:         prev = _alnum_words(" ".join(tokens[k] for k in before))[-2:]
456:         nxt = _alnum_words(" ".join(tokens[k] for k in after))[:2]
457:         own = _alnum_words(" ".join(tokens[a:b]))
458:         lone = not before and not after      # the name is the whole list item
459:         # an isotope label anywhere in the name ("A33 124i"), or just before or after it
460:         # ("[89.Zr] trastuzumab", "trastuzumab 89 - Zr"); an isotope-shaped name counts
461:         # as a name only when it stands alone ("566C", "18f"), never as the substance
462:         # of a longer phrase ("18F fluorothymidine")
463:         inside = [own[i:j] for i in range(len(own)) for j in (i + 1, i + 2) if j <= len(own)]
464:         # and every run of the name's words glued as the key glues them, so a symbol
465:         # split by punctuation inside a stored alias is still an isotope ("[89Z.r]-DFO-")
466:         for i in range(len(own)):
467:             for j in range(i + 1, len(own) + 1):
468:                 glued = "".join(own[i:j])
469:                 if len(glued) > 6:
470:                     break
471:                 if j - i > 1:
472:                     inside.append([glued])
473:         if lone and T.is_isotope_label("".join(own)):
474:             inside = []                      # the whole item is one isotope-shaped name
475:         name = " ".join(tokens[a:b])
476:         # the neighbours glued back together the way the lookup key glues them, so a
477:         # suffix or an isotope spelt in pieces is still seen ("a - w w b", "[89Z.r]")
478:         nxt_all = _alnum_words(" ".join(tokens[k] for k in after))[:8]
479:         prev_all = _alnum_words(" ".join(tokens[k] for k in before))[-8:]
480:         for n in range(1, len(nxt_all) + 1):
481:             glued = "".join(nxt_all[:n]).lower()
482:             if glued in T.BIOSIMILAR_FDA_SUFFIXES or T.is_isotope_label(glued):
483:                 return f"{name} ({' '.join(nxt_all[:n])})"
484:             if len(glued) > 6:
485:                 break
486:         for n in range(1, len(prev_all) + 1):
487:             glued = "".join(prev_all[-n:]).lower()
488:             if T.is_isotope_label(glued):
489:                 return f"({' '.join(prev_all[-n:])}) {name}"
490:             if len(glued) > 6:
491:                 break
492:         # a label fused to a neighbouring word, such as a chelator ("DFO89Zr", "89ZrDFO")
493:         for w in nxt_all[:2] + prev_all[-2:]:
494:             if _isotope_edge(w):
495:                 return f"{name} ({w})"
496:         for ws in inside + [prev[-1:], prev, nxt[:1], nxt]:
497:             if ws and T.is_isotope_label(" ".join(ws)):
498:                 return name if ws in inside else f"{name} ({' '.join(ws)})"
499:         # an isotope glued to the name itself ("89zr-trastuzumab" read through "[89Z.r]")
500:         # anything may sit between them (a chelator: "89ZrDFO-daratumumab"), so the rest
501:         # needs only to end, or start, with a name of the same drug
502:         # A medical isotope anywhere in the list item once its letters and digits are run
503:         # together, however it is split or cased ("89-ZrDFO trastuzumab", "3hvinblastine",
504:         # "trastuzumab Ga68"), refuses an unlabelled drug. A drug whose own name carries a
505:         # label (LUTETIUM LU 177 DOTATATE, FLUDEOXYGLUCOSE F 18) is the labelled product.
506:         if (_item_has_isotope(tuple(tokens[k] for k in same))
507:                 and not _labelled(self.dic["drugs"][did]["name"])):
508:             return name
509:         # A one-letter element is left to the case-marked word check above: lower-case
510:         # chemistry ("13-cis retinoic acid") is not carbon-13.
511:         # The drug's name may sit anywhere in the rest ("89Zrpanitumumab-PET-CT"). A
512:         # one-letter element counts where it is written upper-case in the name
513:         # ("3HVINBLASTINE", "A33124I"); lower-case chemistry ("13-cis") does not.
514:         names = [k for k in self._keys_of(did) if len(k) >= 5]
515:         orig = "".join(re.findall(r"[A-Za-z0-9]+", _plain(" ".join(tokens[a:b]))))
516:         if len(orig) != len(key):
517:             orig = key.upper()                   # cannot align: treat as marked
518:         for i in range(2, min(6, len(key))):
519:             for lab, tail, at in ((key[:i], key[i:], i - 1), (key[-i:], key[:-i], len(key) - 1)):
520:                 if not tail or not T.is_isotope_label(lab):
521:                     continue
522:                 elem = re.sub(r"^\d+m?", "", lab)
523:                 if len(elem) == 1 and not orig[at].isupper():
524:                     continue
525:                 if any(k in tail for k in names):
526:                     return name
527:                 # a label closing a stored name of this drug ("A33124I"): the name itself
528:                 # is the labelled product. Only a mass used in medicine counts here, so a
529:                 # development code's salt letter ("NSC-118218H", "DX-8951F") is not a label
530:                 if (lab == key[-i:] and len(tail) >= 2 and key in self._keys_of(did)
531:                         and _medical_isotope(lab)):
532:                     return name
533: 
534:         return None
535: 
536:     def _whole_name(self, text: str, blocked: dict, found: list):
537:         """The whole input is exactly one stored drug name, letter for letter ("CP-358,774",
538:         "(E/Z)-AFATINIB"): read it whole rather than as the list its punctuation suggests.
539: 
540:         Only after every refusal has had its say: nothing the resolver or the list
541:         linker refused, and no product or tracer pattern in the name."""
542:         if blocked or any(e["type"] == "Refused" for e in found):
543:             return None
544:         name = text.strip(" ?!.")
545:         if T.is_distinct_product(name) or T.is_radiotracer(name):
546:             return None             # the list linker above has already applied the rest
547:         key = T.canonical_key(name)
548:         exact = [h for h in (self.dic["index"].get(key) or [])
549:                  if _fold(h["alias"]).strip(" ?!.").lower() == name.lower()]
550:         if not exact or len({h["drug_id"] for h in exact}) != 1:
551:             return None
552:         did = exact[0]["drug_id"]
553:         return {"type": "Drug", "id": did, "label": self.dic["drugs"][did]["name"],
554:                 "matched_text": name, "matched_alias": exact[0]["alias"]}
555: 
556:     def _link(self, question: str) -> list[dict]:
557:         found: list[dict] = []
558:         seen: set = set()
559:         tokens: list[str] = []
560:         clause: list[int] = []          # which list piece each token is in
561:         inner: list[bool] = []          # token inside parentheses
562:         ci, depth = 0, 0
563:         for piece in re.split(r"[,;\uff0c\uff1b\u3001]", question):
564:             for raw in re.split(r"\s+", piece):
565:                 # a slash or bar, glued or free, ends the piece ("bevacizumab - awwb/
566:                 # osimertinib"); it must not hide a product suffix from the guards
567:                 for j, part in enumerate(re.split(r"[/|]", raw)):
568:                     if j:
569:                         ci += 1
570:                     if not part:
571:                         continue
572:                     if re.search(r"\w", part):
573:                         # edge punctuation and wrappers off ("bevacizumab-awwb?",
574:                         # "(bevacizumab-awwb)"); an isotope ("[89Zr]") is checked as a word
575:                         t = re.sub(r"^[^\w]+|[^\w]+$", "", part)
576:                     elif part in ("-", "\u2013", "\u2014", "+", "&"):
577:                         t = part            # "bevacizumab - awwb" needs its dash
578:                     else:
579:                         t = ""
580:                     if t:
581:                         tokens.append(t)
582:                         clause.append(ci)
583:                         inner.append(depth > 0 or part.startswith("("))
584:                     depth = max(0, depth + part.count("(") - part.count(")"))
585:             ci += 1
586: 
587:         # The resolver's refusals apply to questions too: "Mvasi", "liposomal
588:         # doxorubicin" or "generic version of bevacizumab" must never answer as the
589:         # originator molecule. The text is cut into list items at commas, semicolons
590:         # and listing words ("and", "or", "plus", ...). An item that names a distinct
591:         # product or a tracer anywhere has every drug in it refused: which drug a
592:         # qualifier describes is not guessed, so "osimertinib with generic version of
593:         # bevacizumab" refuses both, while "osimertinib and generic version of
594:         # bevacizumab" keeps osimertinib. Targets and companies in the item still link.
595:         seg: list[int] = []
596:         n = 0
597:         for k, t in enumerate(tokens):
598:             # "and" inside parentheses describes one product ("doxorubicin
599:             # (nanoparticle and liposomal formulation)"); it does not list two
600:             if t.lower() in _JOINERS and not inner[k]:
601:                 n += 1
602:                 seg.append(-1)
603:                 continue
604:             if k and clause[k] != clause[k - 1]:
605:                 n += 1
606:             seg.append(n)
607:         refused: set[int] = {k for k, t in enumerate(tokens)
608:                              if T.is_distinct_product(t) or T.is_radiotracer(t)}
609:         drugs = self._drug_spans(tokens, {k for k in range(len(tokens)) if seg[k] < 0}, seg)
610:         dangling = None                 # a qualifier item with no drug, joined by "and"/"or"
611:         prev_end = -1
612:         for sid in sorted(set(seg) - {-1}):
613:             idx = [k for k in range(len(tokens)) if seg[k] == sid]
614:             text = " ".join(tokens[k] for k in idx)
615:             joined = (dangling is not None and idx[0] > prev_end + 1
616:                       and all(seg[k] < 0 for k in range(prev_end + 1, idx[0]))
617:                       and clause[idx[0]] == clause[prev_end])
618:             prev_end = idx[-1]
619:             hit = [k for a, b in drugs if seg[a] == sid for k in range(a, b)]
620:             if _qualified([tokens[k] for k in idx]):
621:                 named = any(k in refused and (
622:                     T.canonical_key(tokens[k]) in self.dic["index"]
623:                     or tokens[k].lower() in T.BIOSIMILAR_BRANDS | T.REFORMULATION_NAMES)
624:                     for k in idx)
625:                 if not hit and not named:
626:                     # "liposomal and nanoparticle formulation of doxorubicin": a bare
627:                     # qualifier carries over a listing word to the next item's drug; a
628:                     # product named by itself ("Mvasi and bevacizumab") does not
629:                     dangling = text
630:                     continue
631:                 dangling = None
632:             elif joined and hit:
633:                 text = f"{dangling} ... {text}"
634:                 dangling = None
635:             else:
636:                 dangling = None if not joined else dangling
637:                 continue
638:             refused.update(hit)
639:             found.append({"type": "Refused", "id": None, "label": text,
640:                           "matched_text": text,
641:                           "why": "names a distinct product or tracer, not "
642:                                  "the originator molecule"})
643: 
644:         matched: set[int] = set()
645:         for size in range(min(5, len(tokens)), 0, -1):
646:             for i in range(len(tokens) - size + 1):
647:                 if (refused | matched) & set(range(i, i + size)):
648:                     continue
649:                 if size > 1 and (len(set(seg[i:i + size])) > 1 or min(seg[i:i + size]) < 0):
650:                     continue            # a name never spans two list items
651:                 window = " ".join(tokens[i:i + size])
652:                 if len(window) < 3:
653:                     continue
654:                 key = T.canonical_key(window)
655: 
656:                 hits = self.dic["index"].get(key)
657:                 # Several words only make a drug name when the name itself has
658:                 # several words. The key drops spaces, so "is a" read as the
659:                 # abbreviation "isa" (isatuximab) in any question or answer.
660:                 if hits and size > 1 and not re.search(r"[\s\-/]", hits[0]["alias"]):
661:                     hits = None
662:                 if hits and len({h["drug_id"] for h in hits}) == 1:
663:                     did = hits[0]["drug_id"]
664:                     why = self._marked(key, did, tokens, seg, i, i + size)
665:                     if why:
666:                         # the name carries a product suffix or sits beside an isotope:
667:                         # refuse it, never fall back to the bare molecule
668:                         found.append({"type": "Refused", "id": None, "label": why,
669:                                       "matched_text": why,
670:                                       "why": "names a distinct product or tracer, not "
671:                                              "the originator molecule"})
672:                         refused.update(range(i, i + size))
673:                         continue
674:                     if ("Drug", did) not in seen:
675:                         seen.add(("Drug", did))
676:                         found.append({"type": "Drug", "id": did,
677:                                       "label": self.dic["drugs"][did]["name"],
678:                                       "matched_text": window,
679:                                       "matched_alias": hits[0]["alias"]})
680:                     matched.update(range(i, i + size))   # words of a longer name are not read again

codex
350개 테스트와 기존 36개 데모는 통과했습니다. 다만 `trastuzumab conjugated to IRDye800CW`와 `trastuzumab conjugated to DM1`도 거부 없이 원형 약물의 지도와 근거를 반환합니다. 원문 논문과 FDA 문서로 별도 접합체라는 점을 확인했으며, 기존 재현 결과와 함께 차단 항목으로 검증하고 있습니다.

245:                                      "/api/structured", "/api/explore"):
246:                     return self._send(404, b"{}", "application/json")
247:                 with engine.lock:
248:                     if self.path == "/api/explore":
249:                         res = engine.explore(text)
250:                     elif self.path == "/api/resolve":
251:                         res = engine.resolve(text)
252:                     elif self.path == "/api/ask":
253:                         res = engine.ask(text)
254:                     elif self.path == "/api/structured":
255:                         answer = str(body.get("answer") or "").strip()[:20000]
256:                         if not answer:
257:                             raise ValueError("empty answer")
258:                         res = engine.structured(text, answer)
259:                     else:
260:                         answer = str(body.get("answer") or "").strip()[:4000]
261:                         if not answer:
262:                             raise ValueError("empty answer")
263:                         res = engine.check(text, answer)
264:                 self._send(200, json.dumps(res, ensure_ascii=False,
265:                                            allow_nan=False).encode("utf-8"),
266:                            "application/json; charset=utf-8")
267:             except Exception as e:  # show the reason on the page, not a dead request

FILE src/textrules.py
136: # A biosimilar, a nanoparticle/liposomal/pegylated reformulation, or a
137: # sponsor-suffixed version is a separate regulatory product from the
138: # originator. The molecule name is the same; the asset is not.
139: RE_DISTINCT_PRODUCT = re.compile(
140:     r"\bbio[\s\-]?similar\b"
141:     r"|\bbio[\s\-]?betters?\b"
142:     r"|\bfollow[\s\-]?on\s+biologic\b"
143:     r"|\bnab[\s\-]"
144:     r"|\bnanoparticle\s+albumin[\s\-]?bound\b"
145:     r"|\balbumin[\s\-]?bound\b"
146:     r"|\bnano[\s\-]?particulat\w*\b|\bnanoparticle\s+paclitaxel\b"
147:     r"|\boral\s+azacitidine\b"
148:     r"|\bliposomal\b|\bpegylated\b|\bpeg(?:ylated)?[\s\-]r?h?[a-z]{1,6}\b"
149:     r"|\bgeneric\s+version\b"
150:     r"|\b(?:injection|solution|product)\s+of\s+[a-z]+mab\b",
151:     re.I,
152: )
153: 
154: # "Bevacizumab-Pfizer", "Trastuzumab-dkst": a hyphenated sponsor name or an
155: # FDA four-letter suffix on an INN stem.
156: RE_BIOSIMILAR_SUFFIX = re.compile(
157:     r"^[A-Za-z]{6,}(?:mab|cept|tinib|ase|grastim|stim|poetin)\s*[\-–]\s*(?P<suffix>[A-Za-z]{2,12})$", re.I)

+# Independent relation-map review — round 23
+
+Reviewed **`a7d5f7ff65ff4b3634908402a8a4432a4b1353b6`** on 2026-11-04 (Asia/Seoul). **CHANGES REQUIRED.** The three exact M22 blockers are fixed, but two blocking mechanisms remain: an embedded **symbol-first** isotope still escapes all refusal stages, and explicit optical-dye / cytotoxic-payload conjugate descriptions still draw the unmodified antibody. Both reproduce through the original CLI HTTP server and the actual page callbacks. The new positive leaks also existed with HEAD^; they are incomplete coverage, not newly introduced positive-link regressions.
+
+No tracked or application file was changed. New scripts, temporary files and outputs are confined to `audit_evidence_claims/m23/`. The pre-existing brief/log were preserved. The user-recorded over-refusals and ChEMBL bevacizumab synonym limitation do not determine this verdict.
+
+## Method and independently checked claims
+
+Repair notes were treated as hypotheses. Historical **FIXED / NOT FIXED** labels below apply to each exact reproduction, not every spelling of its defect class. Earlier scripts were reused with provenance and output-path adaptations; M2's partial work and completed M2b are included. A newly introduced refusal can close a reproduction without repairing its underlying isotope parser, and those cases are explicitly distinguished.
+
+- **350 tests passed in 43.12 s**, exit 0. The requested pytest command was run with bytecode disabled and `--basetemp` inside this round. [Output](contract/pytest.txt), [command/environment](contract/pytest_summary.json).
+- **6,469 live centres / 22 relation-direction-type-role combinations / zero independent-oracle errors**. **19 DB assertions, 24 UI assertions and four additional self-loop/unknown-type/retirement checks** pass. [Oracle](contract/db_validate.json), [DB assertions](contract/db_assertions.json), [UI assertions](contract/ui_assertions.json), [extra fixtures](contract/self_loop_unknown.json).
+- **36/36 ordinary demos** agree between direct Engine and original CLI HTTP; actual JavaScript callbacks pass the 36 examples and six map examples. [HTTP](contract/demo_http.json), [prior comparison](contract/demo_previous_comparison.json), [callbacks](contract/ui_m3_checks.json).
+- The new attack suite has **87 current/HEAD^ case entries** plus **four supplemental negation entries** (overlapping inputs are not summed as unique cases). Its **348 original CLI HTTP requests all return 200**. Assertions independently establish **17 leaking variants**, including complete map equality and both complete QA evidence-array equalities with the corresponding bare alias; **30 refusal controls** have no centre and zero QA evidence. [Stages](attacks/new_results.json), [HTTP](checks/independent_m23_http.json), [assertions](attacks/assertions.json), [negation supplement](attacks/negation_results.json).
+- The actual form callback renders all **87 HTTP map captures** without render errors. The blocker inputs produce a parent SVG and `CHEMBL1201585` trail. [Page captures](contract/ui_m23_http.json).
+
+The page harness runs the actual JavaScript in a Node VM with controlled DOM/fetch stubs. It tests generated markup, callbacks and state, not browser painting or browser-executed XSS. HEAD^ comparisons replace only `query.py` against the current other modules/data, not an entire historical deployment. Cost numbers are host observations. No model was called.
+
+## M23-B1 — symbol-first isotope inside a larger word still draws the parent (P1)
+
+**Exact inputs: `DOTACe134 trastuzumab` and `trastuzumab DOTAMn52`.** Both return **TRASTUZUMAB / CHEMBL1201585**, with **13 map trials**, **12 trial QA rows** and **12 sponsor QA rows**. Each complete HTTP map and each complete evidence array equals the appropriate bare-trastuzumab result. Reversing the phrase, adding a trailing chelator (`DOTACe134DOTA trastuzumab`), using `DOTAMn52m`, and using Herceptin corroborate the same mechanism. [All-stage traces](attacks/new_results.json), [HTTP](checks/independent_m23_http.json), [equality assertions](attacks/assertions.json), [page rendering](contract/ui_m23_http.json).
+
+Paired controls **`DOTA-Ce134 trastuzumab`**, **`DOTA134Ce trastuzumab`**, **`Ce134DOTA trastuzumab`**, **`DOTA52Mn trastuzumab`** and **`DOTA-Ce-134 trastuzumab`** all refuse. The issue is specifically the position of a symbol-first pair inside a word; the exact M22 mass-first cases are fixed.
+
+**Cause:** the new symbol-first expression at `src/query.py:268` is `(?<![a-z])([a-z]{2})(\d{1,3})(?!\d)`. Its negative lookbehind rejects the `Ce134` part of `dotace134` because another letter precedes it. `Ce134DOTA` matches at the start. The corresponding mass-first expression at `src/query.py:264-267` does allow preceding letters. Ce/Mn exist in `_NAME_MASS` but not the older `_MEDICAL_MASS` substring scanner (`src/query.py:124-136,144-158`); the neighbour/edge fallback uses an element set that also lacks them (`src/textrules.py:290-294`). Thus no later stage rescues the missed pair. The audit records the regex matches and actual stage outcomes, rather than inferring the failure solely from the source. [Assertions](attacks/assertions.json), [independent second probe](history/spec_probe.json).
+
+**Identity evidence:** Bailey et al. prepared and imaged `[134Ce]Ce-DOTA-Trastuzumab`. The compact audit input `DOTACe134` is a reviewer-generated notation variant of that labelled material, **not** a verbatim published name. The primary study supports the distinction from unmodified trastuzumab; it does not justify attaching the originator's graph evidence to the conjugate. [Primary study, DOI 10.1016/j.nucmedbio.2022.04.007](https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/abs/pii/S0969805122000373).
+
+Required result: refuse the modified-product description or resolve the correct distinct product. A regression check should pair symbol-first **start / interior / end** positions with mass-first controls and the documented ordinary-text exceptions. No application repair was made in this audit.
+
+## M23-B2 — explicit nonradioactive conjugates still inherit the originator graph (P1)
+
+**Exact inputs: `trastuzumab conjugated to IRDye800CW` and `trastuzumab conjugated to DM1`.** Both pass every refusal stage and produce the complete bare-trastuzumab map, **13 map trials / 12 trial QA rows / 12 sponsor QA rows**, over original CLI HTTP and actual page callbacks. `IRDye800CW-conjugated trastuzumab`, `trastuzumab IRDye800CW conjugate`, `trastuzumab covalently linked to DM1`, `trastuzumab DM1 conjugate` and Herceptin variants corroborate the same missing product-descriptor guard. [Stages](attacks/new_results.json), [HTTP](checks/independent_m23_http.json), [full-equality assertions](attacks/assertions.json), [page captures](contract/ui_m23_http.json).
+
+This groups the optical-dye and cytotoxic-payload examples as **one descriptor-coverage mechanism**, not two independently counted code defects. M22 already recorded related IRDye/DM1/ADC strings as unadjudicated diagnostics. This round supplies explicit conjugation inputs and primary identity evidence; these were never user-accepted exceptions.
+
+**Identity evidence:** Jeong et al. describe covalently constructing a trastuzumab–IRDye800CW optical imaging probe and evaluating the resulting conjugate. The query explicitly names that modification. [Primary study, DOI 10.5230/jgc.2021.21.e18](https://jgc-online.org/DOIx.php?id=10.5230%2Fjgc.2021.21.e18). The FDA's KADCYLA description identifies trastuzumab covalently linked to DM1 via MCC as an antibody-drug conjugate, and its dosing section explicitly distinguishes it from trastuzumab. A phrase saying only “conjugated to DM1” need not uniquely identify that approved linker/product, but it still cannot designate the unconjugated parent. [FDA prescribing information, sections 11 and 2.2](https://www.accessdata.fda.gov/drugsatfda_docs/label/2026/125427s125lbl.pdf). These are identity premises, not treatment recommendations or claims of clinical benefit. [Source notes and limits](attacks/sources.json).
+
+**Cause:** `RE_DISTINCT_PRODUCT` covers biosimilar/reformulation descriptors but no conjugated/covalently-linked payload construction (`src/textrules.py:139-152,202-212`). `_qualified` delegates to those product/tracer predicates (`src/query.py:315-337`). The new label check recognizes the enumerated radioactive stems and exact words `labelled`, `labeled`, `labelling`, `labeling`, `tagged`, but not these nonradioactive conjugation descriptions (`src/query.py:178-199,227-236`). The resolver yields CONTAINED_ALIAS plus UNRESOLVED residue; `_resolver_blocked` remains empty, `_marked` returns None, and the parent is emitted at `src/query.py:662-679`.
+
+Controls `IRDye800CW-labelled trastuzumab` and `trastuzumab tagged with IRDye800CW` correctly refuse. Merely replacing “labelled” by the equally explicit “conjugated to” loses the protection. `trastuzumab-DM1`, `ado-trastuzumab emtansine` and `Kadcyla` do **not** draw the parent in the exercised standalone calls, so they are not counted as leaking controls. The latter two are ambiguous in the current dictionary. No automatic choice of a specific ADC is required to fix this failure: refusal is sufficient.
+
+## Common execution trace
+
+For each principal blocker input, the actual current calls show:
+
+| Stage | Observed result |
+|---|---|
+| Intervention resolver | CONTAINED_ALIAS + UNRESOLVED; no BLOCKED component |
+| `_resolver_blocked` | `{}` |
+| `_qualified` | `False` |
+| `_item_has_isotope` | `False` |
+| `_marked` | Called for the parent; returns `None` |
+| `_whole_name` | `None` |
+| Public linker | Drug CHEMBL1201585; no Refused entity |
+| `POST /api/explore` | Entire map equals the corresponding bare-alias map |
+| Trial/sponsor `POST /api/ask` | Entire evidence arrays equal the parent's, 12 rows each |
+| Actual page form callback | Parent SVG and CHEMBL1201585 trail; no refusal |
+
+The map selects the accepted hit at `src/app.py:88-95`. This is **not** a case where the map discards an existing Refused entity; every guard misses the qualification. Both blockers are inherited with HEAD^ and independently fail the original product-identity approval bar.
+
+## Repair controls and should-fix observations
+
+The new mass-first Mn parser recognizes `52Mn` and `52mMn`; compact mass-first Ce/Mn, astatinated/radiohalogen words, an arbitrary `radiofutureword`, bare labelled/labeled/tagged and later labels after a negated label all refuse in the tested cases. `non-labelled radiofluorinated trastuzumab`, `non-radio-labelled astatinated trastuzumab`, `non-tagged labelled trastuzumab` and the reverse label order all have no map centre or QA evidence. An earlier negation does not hide the later positive label. Bare/non-labelled controls, `radiotherapy`, `radiology`, `radiofrequency`, ordinary doses/schedules, and CR/CO/SE/LA space exceptions retain their expected readings. [All 87 cases](attacks/new_summary.json), [30 checked refusals](attacks/assertions.json).
+
+**M23-S1 — new negation over-refusal, nonblocking:** `non-radio-astatinated trastuzumab` now refuses, while `non-radioastatinated trastuzumab` still draws the parent. HEAD^ accepts both. The negation loop only recognizes `_LABEL_WORDS`, `_LABELLED` and `_LABEL_STEMS` when extending the negated run (`src/query.py:218-224`); generic `radioastatinated` is recognized only later by the separate radio fallback. In the split spelling it marks only `radio` as negated, and the remaining `astatinated` triggers its own stem. The separate positive-label controls still refuse correctly. [Four-case current/previous stage replay](attacks/negation_results.json). This is a recall/negation-consistency issue, not a parent substitution blocker; it is not silently added to the user's accepted exceptions.
+
+The user-recorded M5-S2, rapamycin forms, `Trastuzumab--vcmmae conjugate`, `9-amino-20(s)-camptothecin`, `il-6ra`, `TAK-659F`, `GS-11CAL-101`, `3en3hg4wsw`, same-list-item over-refusal and ChEMBL bevacizumab development-code synonyms remain separately recorded and **do not determine the verdict**. Other historical residuals remain visible in the matrices; age alone does not make an unresolved observation accepted.

"""Assemble the report from independently produced, currently executed evidence."""
import json
import posixpath
import re
from pathlib import Path

OUT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
def read(name):
    return (OUT/name).read_text(encoding='utf8')

def rebase(text, folder):
    def sub(m):
        target=m.group(1)
        if re.match(r'^(?:[a-z]+:|/|#)',target): return m.group(0)
        return ']('+posixpath.normpath(folder+'/'+target)+')'
    return re.sub(r'\]\(([^\s)]+)\)',sub,text)

+# Independent relation-map review — round 23
+
+Reviewed **`a7d5f7ff65ff4b3634908402a8a4432a4b1353b6`** on 2026-11-04 (Asia/Seoul). **CHANGES REQUIRED.** The three exact M22 blockers are fixed, but two blocking mechanisms remain: an embedded **symbol-first** isotope still escapes all refusal stages, and explicit optical-dye / cytotoxic-payload conjugate descriptions still draw the unmodified antibody.
